| E245 | 2026-10-18 | X:$48293 is the DSP's identity, and the audio-ring control words are the other DSP's (static, DSP listings; closes E236's "who composes them"). The word is written once, in the reset code 0x51fb6-0x51fbb: bit 15 of the HI08 port's GPIO data register (M_HDR) after the port is enabled - a strap pin, never changed afterwards (the only store in the image; X and Y above 0x40000 are the same SRAM, E234). The TI2 has two DSPs running this one image; the flag selects the ESAI set-up (0x51f48 vs 0x51f4d, ESAI_1 clock bits 0x51fd9-0x51fdb) and about a dozen behaviours. The exchange in the audio ring (E236) is symmetric: each tick a DSP writes 7 status words at column 5 of every second frame (its stamp clock first) and 0x123456+n command words at column 5 of the odd frames 128 frames ahead (pointer X:$53c55, writers 0x53d31/0x53d4b/0x53d68/0x53dd7/0x53de2/0x53dfb), and reads the same layout at column 11 (status) and 27 (commands, dispatch table 0x53c69, 6 entries). The received word 0 + 0x40000 becomes the clock only on the DSP whose flag is clear (0x53cb8-0x53cbe); the DSP with the flag set keeps its own clock. So the master DSP owns the stamp clock, the slave takes it from the master's status word with a fixed lead of 256 frames (the link's round trip through the ring, 0x53d73 subtracts the same 256 << 10 from a received command payload), and the constants 0xedc987 (marker, written by the DSP at 0x53934/0x53e85/0x53ead) and 0x123456 are DSP-to-DSP - they are not in the USB chip's or host CPU's code because those never see them. Command 0x12345b carries a channel message (status, data1, data2: sender 0x53df8 from the channel-message handlers via 0x52cab, receiver 0x53e07 -> 0x52cad): the master forwards MIDI channel messages to the slave through the audio ring; the slave's notes are therefore at the master's timing plus the link. The unit: both clocks advance by the block (y:$16 = 64 frames) per tick on the DSP's own frame clock and the master's is reset by the sync ping (E246), nothing outside the DSPs feeds it - one stamp unit is one sample frame at the Virus' rate (consistent with the frame-exact measurement, protocol.md). Which ESAI carries the link is not pinned down: the ESAI handlers at P:$d1f/$d3f/$d5e use columns 0, 2, 3, 6, 7, 9, 12-14 and the ESAI_1 fast interrupts (P:$70/$78) two words at r7 each, so columns 4/5 and 10/11 belong to ESAI_1 (MED) | DSP listing: 0x51fb6, 0x53c6f-0x53eb1, 0x53df8, P:$d1f-$d6f, P:$70-$79 | work/firmware/out/dsp_X_04f000.asm, dsp_P_000000.asm | HIGH (identity flag, symmetric exchange, master/slave clock, 0x12345b forwarding), HIGH (unit = sample frame), MED (ESAI_1) |
| E246 | 2026-10-18 | The stamp clock's life: who resets it, how stamps are compared, and that nothing outside the DSP moves it (static, DSP listing; closes "X:$48293 clock freeze" and bears on E243). The sync ping (`F0 00 20 33 01 dev 73 00 22 vv F7`, protocol.md) is parsed by the DSP itself (SysEx handler 0x5255b; the page-0x73 parameter table at Y:$48054, two words per parameter: 0x22 -> 0x55948, 0x23 -> 0x540ca, 0x1a-0x1e -> 0x5197f/0x50eec/0x50ef0/0x52226/0x5211a, most others the ignore handler 0x519b0). Handler 0x55949: value < 0x7E -> clock X:$55865 = 0 and the current stamp X:$55868 = 0 on BOTH DSPs; the slave (flag clear, E245) also renders the marker: it loads a 7-word voice template (0x18e) and runs the generator 0x55395 with the table 0x5593c once (the single sample on USB 2 that the plug-in finds, protocol.md), then restores template 6. Value 0x7E installs a per-tick hook (X:$5595e = 0x5596a, called from the stamp-queue tick 0x5588a) that keeps rendering through 0x505dc into the 64-frame output buffers 0x4c200/0x4c240 (a continuous test signal, slave only); 0x7F removes the hook. The compare (0x558f0-0x5591f): stamp - clock is taken as a signed 24-bit difference of frames << 10, so the window is modular: a stamp up to 8191 frames ahead of the clock is "future" (queued when more than the threshold ahead, else played at once), one up to 8192 behind is "late" (played at once, clock - 1 becomes the current stamp, no report to the host). Only three things write the clock: the reset above, the tick advance (0x5588b-0x55892, + block), and on the slave the master's status word (0x53cbe). No host-CPU command, no USB event and no audio-stream condition touches it. Consequence for E243/E244: the Virus' clock cannot have "moved" by itself during a Mac export - the plug-in's ring lost frames (E249) | DSP listing 0x55949-0x55984, 0x558f0-0x5591f, Y:$48054-$48140 | work/firmware/out/dsp_X_04f000.asm, dsp_X_04692d.asm | HIGH (reset path, modular compare, writers of the clock), MED (0x7E = test signal: the generator was not followed into the audio) |
| E247 | 2026-10-18 | F2 (song position) has no part in the DSP's stamp handling; the old "without F2 the stamps are ignored" (protocol.md, 2026-10-13) is explained by E246's modular window (static). The F2 handler 0x540a6-0x540c8 stores the position as 24-ppqn ticks (position in sixteenths x 6 - 1 -> Y:$53eb9), sets Y:$53eba = 0xFF, and calls 0x5311c (copies the tick accumulator Y:$53eb7/8 and the tempo values Y:$53ec0/1 to the clock-synced modulation state X/Y:$b54-$b58) and 0x53141 (arpeggiator/LFO phase Y:$48573) - the DSP's internal clock sync (arp, LFO, delay), nothing near the stamp clock, the threshold or the queue. FA/FB/FC do the same with the position reset (0x54081, 0x5408f, 0x5409a). The DSP evaluates the stamped F8 ticks for its tempo (0x54008 -> 0x5404c: (current stamp - clock) >> 10). Why the 2026-10-13 experiments correlated with F2: before the marker was known the stamps were ring-relative, so the Virus' clock sat at a random offset per session; a send-ahead of 2000-5000 frames is "future" (honoured) only when the offset leaves it inside the 8191-frame window, about 44 % of sessions, and "late" otherwise - the honoured/immediate runs were session luck, not F2 (3 honoured runs with F2, 1/3 without, protocol.md). With the marker the window question is settled (stamps are clock-relative) and F2/FE at the anchor remain useful only for the Virus' own tempo sync and the watchdog (E237) | DSP listing 0x540a6-0x540c8, 0x5311c-0x5317a, 0x54008-0x54080 | work/firmware/out/dsp_X_04f000.asm | HIGH (what F2 touches), MED (the old observation's explanation) |
| E248 | 2026-10-18 | Rule-2 hang on the DSP side, reassessed (static). (1) The live byte ring A (128 words, E233) is drained completely every tick (0x522be loop, no per-tick cap) and only held while queued stamped bytes play (gate X:$5586a, cleared when the queue runs dry or the parser is between messages); it overflows only above ~88 kB/s within one 1.45 ms tick - unreachable through the USB chip and host CPU (E229/E231), so a flood does not corrupt it. (2) DEBUGcc traps halt the core: on a DSP56300 the instruction enters OnCE debug mode and waits for a debugger, i.e. the DSP stops producing audio until a reset; the host CPU writes the host port without a ready check (E230), so it then blocks too - the whole Virus freezes, panel included (E241's symptom). The 16 trap sites (E234) are reachable from MIDI only indirectly: the pool allocator 0x53b0f (first-fit over a free list X:$53b03/$53b04, total X:$53b06; trap 0x53b19 when no block fits) is used by the part/patch set-up (0x56006 and 18 more sites allocate voice/effect structures with templates from X:$45c80; the only frees are the teardown 0x551d8-0x55203), not per note - a flood does not drain it; the per-part counter trap 0x55fb4 guards a count the code itself decrements. (3) The stamped-event queue (2046 bytes, no fill check, E238) remains the only MIDI-driven corruption: once it wraps, the parser 0x522de receives arbitrary bytes - including 0xF4, which selects the host CPU's private command handler 0x5256c (sub-commands by data byte: part selection 0x525e0, mode switches 0x525ce/0x525d3, state machine Y:$482e4; E236) - so a wrapped queue can issue random host commands to the synth engine; a halt through one of the traps or a mis-set state is then plausible but not proven. The practical rule stands: bound the stamped traffic (sendbudget.h) and never send F4 or the Multi "Original Patch" 72 00 03 (page 0x72 param 3 is handled by the host CPU's multi parser, not analysed) | DSP listing 0x522be-0x522dd, 0x53b0f-0x53b7e, 0x55fa6-0x55fb8, 0x5256c-0x5262d | work/firmware/out/dsp_X_04f000.asm | HIGH (ring drain, trap semantics, allocator use), MED (queue wrap -> F4 path), LOW (which trap halts) |
| E249 | 2026-10-18 | Mechanism for the Mac export clock shift (E243/E244), from the firmware reading (E246: the Virus' clock has no external input) plus our code: on macOS the plug-in's audio ring is filled by a Core Audio IOProc (native/src/device/audioin.cpp io_proc) that appends every buffer it gets and ignores the input time stamp. Core Audio skips IO cycles when the system is overloaded (a Live export renders at full CPU and the IOProc runs in Live's process) and reports them only through the next cycle's inInputTime.mSampleTime; each skipped cycle shortens the ring by the device's buffer size without a trace, while the Virus' clock runs on. The plug-in stamps events as ring frame + lead - marker, so after a skipped 5600 frames (eleven 512-frame cycles) every stamp lands 5600 frames behind the Virus' clock: late, played on arrival, "sync errors" until the next power cycle or marker - the K drop from ~6100 to ~511 in E243 and the same in 0.8.25 (E244: the IOProc code is older than the paced render). Linux does not show it because the broker's libusb reader fills USB gaps with silence against the wall clock (vtiusbaudio_libusb.c, "stream stalled, N ms filled with silence"; the Linux journal shows these at every wake, 19:21:56 and 19:35:07-11 on 2026-10-18, never during a render). Fix (branch round-0.8.27, not on hardware yet): native/src/device/gapfill.h follows the device's sample time - the frames between the ring's length and sample_time - origin become silence before the buffer (gaps up to 5 s; a backwards or longer jump re-bases, the StallWatch handles sleep); io_proc applies it when kAudioTimeStampSampleTimeValid is set; "filled N" is new in the plug-in's stats line. Unit tests tests/test_gapfill.cpp (in step, 10 skipped cycles -> 5120 frames, fractional times, device restart, 19 s sleep); vtitests 242/242 and the plug-in build on Linux; the macOS part compiles only there (CI). Hardware check: Live export on the Mac, then klog K before and after the export equal, "filled" > 0 during the export, no sync error page. Residual on Linux/macOS broker streams (LOW, not seen): the broker's filler acts only when 30 ms or more are missing, so up to 30 ms of ring slip can persist | audioin.cpp io_proc (before this change), E243/E244 logs, Linux broker journal | native/src/device/audioin.cpp, gapfill.h, tests/test_gapfill.cpp; journalctl --user -u tirevive-broker.service | MED (mechanism: fits every observation, not yet confirmed by a "filled" count on the Mac), HIGH (the Virus side is not the cause, E246) |
| E250 | 2026-10-18 | What the DSP tells the host CPU, and the origin of the Sync Information Event (73 00 54 01) is still open (static). DSP -> host CPU goes through the HI08 transmit ring (640 words at X:$4c800, write X:$29 / read X:$2a; 0x523b9 queues one byte and enables HTIE, the ISR 0x52396 drains it; the timer-0 ISR P:$da1 exchanges one word each way per tick as well). Senders found: (a) 0x531bb, run every tick from 0x53105, recomputes a beat-phase flag X:$48572 from the tick accumulator and, when it changes, sends 'F5 <0|1>' (plus a part update 0x56189 on 1) - the tempo indicator, not an error; (b) 0x53180 sends a status word with 'F8 00 00' when the clock-synced state Y:$b56 crosses zero; (c) 0x5503f composes a SysEx-shaped block 'F0 00 20 33 00 00 0B 00 <5 bytes> F7' in 4-byte link frames (header word = X:$480c9 << 4 | 0x40000); (d) 0x529cd/0x5254x send parameter dumps framed by 0xF4 0x00 0xF4. None carries 0x54. On the host CPU the SysEx builder for the pages 0x6e-0x74 (FUN_CODE_654a: F0 00 20 33 01 dev page param value F7) is called only with page 0x74 (FUN_CODE_085e), and FUN_CODE_6511 (same pages) only with 0x73 and parameters 0x00/0x01/0x55/0x7f; the 0x54 report is composed elsewhere (a table-driven sender, or the value 0x54 is computed) - open. Working hypothesis (LOW): the host CPU reports it when the DSP's beat flag (a) toggles while the stamped F8 ticks are late (E243: ~3 reports/s with every FE/F8 late) | DSP listing 0x523b9, 0x52396, 0x531bb, 0x53180, 0x5503f, 0x529cd; host bank00 FUN_CODE_654a/6511/085e | work/firmware/out/dsp_X_04f000.asm, bank00.txt, bank06.txt | HIGH (the channels and senders), open (0x54) |
| E251 | 2026-10-18 | Origin of the Sync Information Event `F0 00 20 33 01 10 73 00 54 01 F7` (closes E250's open point; static, DSP + host CPU listings). The DSP composes it itself: the MIDI-clock handler (F8, 0x54008 -> 0x5404c) compares the newest received time stamp (X:$55868, kept at max(newest stamp, clock - 1) by 0x558cf) with its stamp clock; while the stamp is ahead the tick is "on time" (0x54056-0x54074, which also re-arms the continuation Y:$53ecf = 0x54075); the first tick after that whose newest stamp is behind the clock runs 0x54075: it stores the tick time, sets the continuation to 0x54011 (further late ticks are silent) and calls 0x5411f, which fills page 0x73, part 0 (0x40 when Y:$48467 is set; it is 0), parameter 0x54, value 1 and jumps to the DSP's SysEx sender 0x52484. That routine writes the whole message as USB-MIDI packets into the host-port ring (0x523b9): a header word per packet = cable X:$480cb (= 2 in the data record) << 4 | CIN 4, the last packet CIN 6, bytes F0 00 20 / 33 01 <Y:$482ed = 0x10> / 73 00 54 / 01 F7. On the host CPU the INT0 interrupt (vector 0x0003 -> FUN_2e80 -> FUN_2644; INT0 = the DSP's host request) reads the first byte: 0xF5 = beat flag (bit _7_2, the LED pattern FUN_5c63/FUN_5c05 with global 0x32), 0xF4 = a DSP parameter report (page, param, value -> a parameter record with page | 0x80), anything else = a 4-byte USB-MIDI frame copied unchanged into the USB transmit queue 0x6C00-0x6FFF (columns 0x6C-0x6F) while the plug-in link is up (_4_4), so the DSP's SysEx reaches the PC on cable 2 of EP 0x86 exactly as captured. Meaning: the Virus reports once each time its newest stamp falls behind its clock at a clock tick after having been ahead - "the stamps have caught up with real time", one event per transition; a steady stream of late stamps gives one event, a lead that hovers around zero gives several per second (E074: 15/s, E243: 3-6/s with the stamped FE every 300 ms moving X:$55868). Late stamps themselves still play (E246); the event is the symptom of a lead margin that is too small, not of a lost note. Other DSP reports use the same sender: 0x52453 turns page-0x70 changes into CCs (status 0xB0) and page-0x71 into 0xA0 messages while X:$4810f / X:$48111 are set (both 1), everything else into SysEx; 0x5503f sends an internal message `F0 00 20 33 00 00 0B 00 <5 bytes> F7` on cable X:$480c9 (= 1, the MIDI port EP 0x85; meaning open) | DSP listing 0x54008-0x54080, 0x5411f-0x5412b, 0x52484-0x524cf, 0x52453; data X:$480cb/$480c9, Y:$482ed/$48467; host bank00 FUN_2e80, FUN_2644 (asm via DisasFuncs.java), FUN_5c63 | work/firmware/out/dsp_X_04f000.asm, dsp/rec/X_04692d.bin, f_bank00*.txt | HIGH |
| E252 | 2026-10-18 | The host CPU's parameter model (static, bank00 asm + bank02 tables; explains how panel, PC and DSP changes meet). Every change is a 4-byte record (value, part, internal page, parameter) in a queue at XDATA 0x0D00 (write index INTMEM 0x44, read 0x45; FUN_135f appends, FUN_13d3 pushes to the front, FUN_13a1 pops). The main loop's FUN_1102 takes one record: resolves the value through the page-2 tables 0x8d16/0x9025, translates (internal page, parameter) through the page-2 table 0x8700 into the SysEx (page, parameter) pair (FUN_49b9/FUN_49e3: 7 internal pages of up to 128 entries; 0-2 -> single pages 0x70/0x71/0x6e/0x6f, 3 -> multi 0x72 plus 0x6f/0x73 entries, 4 -> global 0x73/0x74, 5 mixed, 6 seven entries; a parameter byte with bit 7 set is never sent), sends the pair to both DSPs as two host-port words 'page F4 F4' and 'part param value' (FUN_6511 -> FUN_6270, pages 0x6e-0x73 only) unless the record came from a DSP (bit 7 on the page byte), queues a page-0x73 change for the PC as a link frame 0x4F, param, value, part (FUN_27f6 into the USB queue; how the USB chip forwards a 0x4F frame was not followed), and stores it through the dispatch table 0xa134 (7 pages x 128 handlers; the reader table is 0xab31, FUN_0826 reads a parameter). Memory: multi part arrays at XDATA 0x4A00 + 0x20 + part (bank) / + 0x30 + part (program) (FUN_03be), the global blocks at 0x0400 (FUN_0172) and 0x0480 (FUN_012a). Examples: internal (3, 0x53/0x54) = multi 0x72 parameters 0x20/0x21 = the part's bank/program - FUN_0331 sets them when a patch is chosen on the panel in multi mode (single mode: internal (3, 0x69/0x6a) = 0x73 0x65/0x66, internal only); internal (4, 0x25) = 0x73 0x54, the host's slot for the sync event (the DSP sends the SysEx itself, E251). | E257 | 2026-10-18 | What the host CPU's 0x4F frames are, and that the USB chip owns the sample rate (closes E252's open point; static, USB chip asm via DisasAt.java on tusbcode.bin + host asm). The USB chip's main loop reads a completed 4-byte frame from the host CPU (INTMEM 0x9e-0xa1, serial ISR 0x13e7) and routes it by the cable nibble: cable 0/1 -> the EP 0x85 IN ring, every other cable -> the EP 0x86 IN ring (the plug-in interface), except cable 4 (0x4x), which is a message to the chip itself: the next byte is a key dispatched through the table at code 0x0616 (FUN_098c), the byte after it the value. Keys are page-0x73 global parameters and two commands: 0x0F "System Clock/SA Freq" -> INTMEM 0x86 = value, 0x87 = 3 (staged); 0x10 "System Clock/Frequency" -> value 0: rate 44100 (0xAC44), 1: rate 48000 (0xBB80), compared with the current rate (0x10b0) and, when different, stored in INTMEM 0x7e/0x7f with the change flag bit 0x07 - the chip switches its audio clock; 0x11 "System Clock/Mode" -> INTMEM 0x84 (0 or 1); 0x1F "Analog Input/Gain" -> INTMEM 0x80 = value & 3; 0x2B "SPDIF/Input Routing" -> INTMEM 0x82 (0 or 1); 0xF5 value 0/1 -> clears/sets bit 0x11 (link mode); 0xF6 -> bytes into a 16-byte buffer (0x9c) then P3.5 (a transfer to the chip's peripheral, open); unknown keys -> bit 0x0e. The host CPU sends such a frame whenever a page-0x73 parameter changes (FUN_1102 -> FUN_27f6, from the panel, the DSP or the PC's SysEx), and at a USB connect it re-sends 0x0F, 0x11, 0x2B and 0x1F (FUN_337a -> FUN_5bf9) - NOT 0x10. Consequence: the Virus' audio sample rate has two masters, the PC's UAC SET_CUR on the endpoints (E241) and the global parameter 0x10 "System Clock/Frequency" (0 = 44.1 kHz, 1 = 48 kHz, the panel's setting); `F0 00 20 33 01 10 73 00 10 00 F7` is the documented way to put the chip to 44.1 kHz from the MIDI side, exactly what the panel does. TI Revive sends neither 0x0F nor 0x10 today (only 0x2D at start and the ping 0x22); the original's start-up globals in our captures are 0x3A/0x3B/0x3C/0x52 (E039). Proposal for the 48 kHz state (E175, E241): when 48 kHz packets appear, send 73 00 10 00 (and read it back with the global request) in addition to SET_CUR - a normal global parameter, no vendor request, rule 2 safe; needs a code change with tests and a hardware check | tusbcode.bin asm 0x0610-0x06f0 (f_tusb_keys.txt), tusbcode.txt 1340-1430; host bank00 FUN_27f6, FUN_337a, FUN_5bf9; XML bank F names 15/16/17/31/43 | work/firmware/out/f_tusb_keys.txt, images/tusbcode.bin | HIGH (routing, keys, rate values), MED (0xF5/0xF6 meaning) |

| E259 | 2026-10-18 | How the Access updater flashes the sectors (closes E256's open point; REA/Ghidra on the MSI custom action CAInstallerHelperDeferred, x86-64, sha256 5452ea54...). FUN_18000f3e0 loads firmware.bin, reads the TABL names and hands vti.bin / vti_snow.bin / vti_2.bin (also accepted: vti2.bin) to FUN_18000fec0 with the model index 0/1/2. That parser IGNORES the 3-byte record addresses: for every sector chunk of 0x8000, 0x8c00 or 0x8c02 bytes it takes the flash bank from the chunk TAG - 'F' and 'P' tags give 0x80 + the decimal number, 'S' tags the number itself (F020 -> bank 0x94, F034 -> 0xA2, F035 -> 0xA3, F060/F061 -> 0xBC/0xBD, P064 -> 0xC0, P120 -> 0xF8, S002 -> 0x02) - strips the addresses (copies the two 32-byte halves of each 70-byte record into a 32 KB bank buffer) and files the bank in a per-model map; a tag with any other first letter fails the parse. The device's flash page is (bank + 0x80) mod 256, which reproduces every address in E228 (F020 -> 0x14, S002 -> 0x82, P120 -> 0x78). The update procedure (FUN_180005ad0: scan the device, compare the firmware version/build with the package, update the Device Information Block = bank 0x80 with 0x60 bytes of device data merged at its end, "Writing firmware" = FUN_180007230 over banks 0x80-0xBD in pairs: erase, write 32 KB at bank*0x8000 (device bank addressing), CRC check per bank; banks 0xC0-0xFD are skipped when their CRC is already intact; then "Writing ROM Banks" FUN_180007900, then mark complete). Consequences: F035 is flashed to page 0x23 (0x230000) although its records say 0x220000 - the flat image vti2_flash.bin (E228's script) placed it wrongly; F060 and F061 are two copies of the USB chip firmware at 0x3C0000 and 0x3D0000 (redundancy, the host picks one - not followed); the "skipped since CRC is intact" rule means the ROM banks are rewritten only when damaged. No host-CPU code selects page 0x23 (no constant page select 0x23, the DSP boot stream stops at F034's remaining count 0, no third host port: XDATA 0x0205 has one unrelated use), so the 2009 "fvd102.lod" data image is flashed but, as far as the static reading goes, unused by OS 5.1.7 on the TI2 - a legacy sector | REA ev_4539a2a1..., ev_4cd5f250..., ev_9a24d324... | E261 | 2026-10-19 | Stamp threshold semantics, residency per platform and the two failure modes (static, DSP listing + the plug-in's send path; corrects E238 and E248). (1) Semantics: a stamped block whose stamp is at most the threshold X:$5586c ahead of the DSP clock is parsed at once, the stamp becomes the current stamp (0x55904) and the note-on handler 0x53337 copies it into the voice's start slot X:$54145 - the voice starts AT the stamp, so the threshold is the maximum per-voice scheduling delay, not "play now". Beyond the threshold the bytes up to the next F5 are parked in the 2046-byte queue (E235). The threshold is 1023 while the counter X:$5412e is 0 (0x55882-0x55888: teq with #$3ff), otherwise a value slewed by 16 per tick towards min(X:$5413b - y:$2a, 1023) (E262: the counter is live part set-up records, so the window only shrinks during a patch change). (2) Residency: a stamp leaves the plug-in A frames ahead of the newest ring frame (timeline + lead - marker, lead = S + A, protocol.md) and needs 512-768 frames to reach the DSP (A = 768 still exact, 512 partly late). Linux/macOS, A = 1024: the stamp is ~256-512 frames ahead when the DSP reads it - within the threshold, nothing is parked, the queue stays empty. Windows, A = 3072: ~2300-2560 ahead, ~1300-1550 frames = 30 ms parked. E238's "every stamped byte sits in the queue for about 160 ms" (from the lead K ~7000) is withdrawn: most of the lead is spent in the ring and the USB path before the DSP sees the stamp. The byte budget (sendbudget.h, 4 kB/s, burst 1000) stays as the safe bound for what the DSP can park within that 30 ms window (< 2046 bytes). E238's "quantitative match" with the 2026-10-13 stress hang is withdrawn too: that hang was on Linux with A = 1024 (nothing parked) and the Virus still answered MIDI - its mechanism is open again. (3) Gaps found in the plug-in and closed in round 0.8.27: unstamped messages on cable 2 (parameter SysEx from the editor, part dumps on a patch change, the state, CC resets) follow the previous stamp and are parked with it on Windows (two patch dumps plus a burst reach the 2046 wrap) and were not counted by the budget -> DeviceOut counts queued cable-2 bytes as a debt capped at one burst (SendBudget::take_queued: a state send of 100 kB delays stamped traffic by 250 ms, not 25 s); automation from process() inherited the previous event's stamp (on Windows up to ~1400 frames off its block time) -> its parameter SysEx gets its own F5 at the block time (stamprule.h; queued messages keep inheriting, a stamp behind the newest one would move X:$55868 and can raise the sync event, E251); no explicit guard against 72 00 03 -> sendguard.h drops page 0x72 parameters 0-3 (Flags, Original Bank, Original Patch; 4-13 = the multi name pass) and status F4 with a log line; the Windows streamer lacked E241's handling -> vtiusbaudio.c sends silent same-size OUT packets while 48 kHz packets arrive and repeats SET_CUR 3x; E257's lever (global 73 00 10 00) is sent by both brokers next to the SET_CUR retry (ratelever.h) and the Virus' own report of the global is logged when it arrives (a read-back request is not in our evidence, open). Tests: vtitests 251/251 (test_sendbudget, test_stamprule, test_sendguard, test_pktstat); plug-in, Linux broker, mingw broker and the classic wrapper build. (4) Two failure modes, to be kept apart: (a) the stress hang (2026-10-13, Linux): audio silent, MIDI and panel still answering - not the stamped queue (nothing parked), cause open; (b) E241 (Linux, after a wake): a loud signal, then the panel dead - the 48 kHz state with a starved output; E248's chain "DSP halts -> host CPU blocks on the host port -> panel dead" is withdrawn (E262: the host writes the port without any ready check and cannot block there), so a dead panel means the host CPU itself stopped, cause open. | E262 | 2026-10-19 | What the DSP's counter X:$5412e counts, where the threshold's input comes from, and that the host CPU writes the host port without a ready check (static; closes round 0.8.27 T7/T8). (1) X:$5412e is reset to 0 in the init 0x5435a (with a dozen neighbours $5412f, $54131-$54134, $54141-$54144; called from the set-up chain 0x53871 -> 0x53881), incremented once at 0x544b3 at the end of 0x54484 - which allocates a record of 0x15 words from the template at X:$1f38 (0x5419e, 0x5442f -> allocator 0x53b71, record address in X:$54135), links it into the list X:$1f43/X:$1f44, gives it a lifetime X:$1f86 = y:$17 and a remaining count X:$1f87 = -1 (= not armed yet) and bumps X:$1f3c/X:$1f3d - and decremented at 0x5457d when a record is retired: from the per-tick walk 0x5443d over the X:$1f3e live records (0x54481, when the record's count X:$1f87 is below X:$543ce) and from the teardown loop 0x54540 that retires every record (0x54544). 0x54484 is called from 0x547f8 (a loop of (X:$55c5c >> 16) + 1 records) and from 0x55fe5, the part set-up path next to the counter trap 0x55fb4 (E248). So X:$5412e = the number of live set-up records: allocated in a burst by the part/patch set-up, retired per tick as their counts run out. The threshold is 1023 only while none is alive: the DSP shortens its scheduling window during a patch change (what one record drives - a voice ramp, a crossfade segment - is not pinned down, LOW). (2) The threshold's input X:$5413b (0x54b1e): 0x600 = 1536 while the flag X:$54144 is set (0x54adb sets it to 0xff, 0x54ae2 counts it down per tick, 0x54af7 re-derives it from y:$2a), else max(2 * y:$2a, 0xc0 = 192) - the mac terms 0x54b28-0x54b2a multiply small integers in fractional mode and reach only the low accumulator word, so they do not change the stored word (MED). The setting X:$54a2c is a value <= 0x40 from X:$548d4 (0x54938-0x54953: a sum of three counters capped at 0x30, else 0x40), applied by 0x54a39 through three piecewise tables at 0x54a7b/0x54a8f/0x54aa6 (rows of threshold, value, handler = 0x54ab6 or 0x56268) and 0x54b1e; y:$2b/y:$2a come from the same setting (0x54b0c/0x54ac1: y:$2b = f(setting), y:$2a = 2 * y:$2b; cleared at 0x54ad3) and are advanced per tick by 0x54af7 (y:$2b += the block, y:$2a halved). With the threshold routine 0x5586e (min(X:$5413b - y:$2a, 1023), slewed by 16 per tick, 1023 while X:$5412e == 0; E261) the practical reading: outside a part set-up the DSP accepts stamps up to 1023 frames ahead as "now, start the voice at the stamp"; during one the window shrinks to a few hundred frames or less, so more of our Windows lead is parked and nothing changes on Linux/macOS (A = 1024, E261). (3) Host port: FUN_605b (a single MIDI byte: ICR 0x39, then 0xFF, 0xFF, byte at the data registers; callers FUN_1ffb x11, FUN_286d x4, FUN_28e7 x2, FUN_2e00) and FUN_6113 / FUN_6270 (a complete 3-byte message, ICR 0x31; callers FUN_2d84, FUN_337a, FUN_63bf / FUN_4ee8, FUN_5953, FUN_6511) are plain stores to XDATA 0x0000-0x0007 AND 0x0100-0x0107 with interrupts disabled for the duration (EA saved and cleared): no status read, no polling loop, no wait. Every word goes to both host ports, i.e. both DSPs (consistent with E245's one image on two chips; which DSP acts on a byte is decided on the DSP side - open). So E248's "the host CPU blocks on the port when the DSP halts" is wrong: the HI08 has no handshake for data writes, the 8051's bus write always completes, a halted DSP loses the bytes and the host CPU, its panel and MIDI thru keep running. E230/E233 stand. Both DSPs receive every byte the host CPU writes (E262: both host ports) and both parse the stream: the sync ping's handler resets X:$55865 and X:$55868 on both before it tests the identity flag (0x5594c-0x55954; only the DSP with the flag clear goes on to render the marker, 0x55955, and the same test gates 0x5596a) - consistent with E245/E246. The voice handlers then execute a message only on one DSP: the note-on handler 0x53337 (0x53352-0x5335a), the handler 0x5346b (reached from the dispatcher 0x521f4) and 0x5ee8a each take the current slot index X:$54136, mask bit 6 (y:$16 = 0x40) and compare it with X:$48293 - a note runs on the master when its slot's bit 6 is clear, on the slave when set; the other DSP returns. The hand-over goes through the audio ring (E245): the sender 0x53d2c-0x53d3c writes command 0x123456 with the slot index X:$54136, the two message words (y1, y0) and the voice start stamp X:$54145 (= the note's time stamp, E261), then allocates a set-up record (0x5419e); the receiver 0x53d51 (first entry of the dispatch table 0x53c69) stores the slot index into X:$54136, looks the slot's record up (0x53d5a, field +2), reads the two words and the stamp, subtracts 0x100 << 10 (256 frames in the clock's format, 0x53d70-0x53d75) into its own X:$54145 and allocates records through 0x547e5 (E262's counter X:$5412e thus also grows on the slave from the master's commands). Slot indices: the set-up loops assign them from 0 (0x5437e, incremented per slot at 0x543ab) and from 0x10 for the 16 parts (0x562b2); the dispatcher 0x54f36/0x54f3c sets X:$54136 per incoming message. Which slots carry bit 6 (i.e. which parts or voices live on the slave) is not pinned down (MED), nor whether the slave discards its own copy of a stream note through the same bit test or never parses it (LOW). Timing reading for T9 (prediction, LOW): the slave's clock runs 256 frames ahead of the master's status word (E245), the forwarded start stamp is reduced by 256, and the slave's audio reaches the master's mix through the ring - if these cancel, a note on a slave part starts at the same output frame as on a master part; a measured ~256-frame offset between two parts would show the split instead. | DSP listing 0x5594c-0x5596e, 0x53352-0x5335a, 0x5346b-0x53475, 0x5ee8a-0x5ee93, 0x53d2c-0x53d3c, 0x53d51-0x53d7d, 0x53c69, 0x5437e-0x543b1, 0x562b2-0x562bd, 0x54f36-0x54f41 | work/firmware/out/dsp_X_04f000.asm | HIGH (bit-6 gate in three handlers, command layout, both clocks reset by the ping), MED (slot numbering), LOW (what runs on the slave, the timing prediction) |

Log: after the project opened the plug-in reported "the Virus' audio stood still (sleep?)" every 2-3 s (14:54:37-14:55:04, more than ten times) and sent the whole state again each time. Cause in the new code: while a dump went out in pieces the worker skipped its regular work (idle work, audio scan, stall watch, presence) for each round; a state holds 16 dumps = ~1.8 s of pieces, so StallWatch (LIMIT_MS 1500) saw the stream move after more than 1.5 s without a check and reported a sleep, and each resent state started the next ~1.8 s of dumps. Fix: the worker only picks the due piece in the selection and writes it after the regular work of the round. Headless check (nativehost, Init state, two runs back to back with each build): no stall report with either build - the headless state goes out before the stream runs, so the false stall did not reproduce there; vtitests 256/256 | user, diag.log 14:54-14:55 | ~/.local/share/virus-ti-native/diag.log | HIGH (cause), open (REAPER redo of the fix) |

The original sends F2 + 73 00 23 01 (device id 00) + 16 x 6F <part> 7F 00 in one write at every Play and loop restart (captures, E274). TI Revive sent F2 only when it anchored its timeline - the arpeggiator and clock-synced LFOs did not restart with the bar. Page D 124-126 are the input block (Input/Mode, Input/Select, Input/Atomizer; Access parameter list), 127 is hidden right after it. The Atomizer handler (6F/7E -> 0x5ee81) gates on the part's voice slot like E263 (X:$54136 bit 6 against X:$48293), keeps the part's Atomizer setting in the per-part word X:(r2+$566b0) and, when it switches between zero and non-zero, rebuilds the part's processing (0x21e, 0x541f6). The handler of 127 (0x565a8) writes the NEXT word of the same per-part record, X:(r2+$566b1), and the word X:$565a7 (default 0x6400), both as v x 0x6400 << 7; both addresses are operands of a compiled processing chain at 0x56570-0x565a7 whose routines include 0x5c344, which writes incoming samples into a circular Y-memory buffer (modulo addressing) - the shape of the Atomizer's input buffer. Reading: parameter 127 is a hidden Atomizer/input value per part that the original sets to 0 at every transport start (a reset of the Atomizer's buffer or sync at Play, MED for "Atomizer", LOW for the exact effect). | E279 | 2026-10-19 | Late start after a render at 48 kHz (E278, user's L3 note) reproduced and fixed. Headless reproduction on the TI2 (nativehost --rate 48000 --block 1024 --notes --transport 2 --offline 10 --offline-end 18 --ahead 300@22, new option --offline-end): note latency steady at 61.4 ms during the render; after it the plug-in re-anchored 15 times in 10 s (the 'jump back to real time' branch for |err| > 512) and the notes came 55.0, 56.4, 58.2 ... 60.9 ms - about 8 s to settle. Cause: a timeline jump moves the direct 44.1 kHz read position at once (read_at follows the target), but the resampled path (host rate != 44.1 kHz) only steers its read position towards the target at <= 0.2 % speed unless the error exceeds 2048 frames; REAPER's ~1000-frame jump after the render took >10 s (the takes' +1800 ppm, E278). Fix: rs_valid_ = false on every anchor and every jump back to real time, so the resampled output jumps with the timeline like the direct one. After the fix, same run: 61.1 ms from the first note after the render on (61.0-61.2 ms to the end); the one note exactly at the switch (18 s) came 17 ms late (in a DAW the transport is stopped there); the 22 s note is the tool's simulated burst in both runs. vtitests 262/262. Static (REA/Ghidra on libSwell, matched line by line against the open WDL source swell-generic-gdk.cpp / swell-dlg-generic.cpp, commit 9c8cab5): the crash function is SWELL_SetCursor (libSwell 0x158500), which calls gdk_window_set_cursor on the global SWELL_focused_oswindow (0x3c0418). SWELL clears that global when it destroys a window through swell_oswindow_destroy (0x150b80), but not on a second path: EndDialog keeps a closed modal dialog's OS window as a "spare" for the next dialog and detaches it from the dialog; a timer (100 ms, 500 ms while the app is inactive) then destroys it in swell_dlg_destroyspare (0x144430) with a bare gdk_window_destroy. Runtime (work/timing/e287-20261020-141122.log, -142238.log, X focus log e287-focus-142319.log): every render's progress window ("Rendering to file...", reused from the "Render Warning" window = the spare path) is destroyed by 0x144430; in the good cases the focus global had moved on before (REAPER refocused the Render to File dialog, or the window manager gave a REAPER window the X focus); in three cases (14:16:10.412, 14:25:27.857, 14:26:38.312) the spare was destroyed while it was still the focused window and nothing reset the global; twice the next cursor update (mouse movement, 2-5.5 s later) crashed with the E265 stack (same addresses), once a REAPER "Finished" window took the focus 27 ms later and rescued it. In all three the X focus had left REAPER during the render (to mutter's no-focus window 0x200003 = a non-X application, e.g. the terminal; SWELL's inactive check gdk_screen_get_active_window logged each time). No frame of TI Revive in any stack; the plug-in's threads idle. Decisive test (user, 14:31, project with ReaSynth only, TI Revive not loaded: no plug-in log line since 14:30, no TI Revive frame in any thread): render started, the user clicked into the terminal during it (X focus to 0x200003 at 14:31:16.079), the spare was destroyed while focused at 14:31:23.249, SIGSEGV at 14:31:26.553 with the identical stack (work/timing/e287-20261020-143006.log). So the crash is REAPER's/SWELL's alone, reproducible without TI Revive; the trigger is another application having the focus when the render window closes; the 'editor open' condition of E287 was a coincidence (the user tended to switch to the terminal while testing with the editor open). | E292 | 2026-10-20 | Program +/- on the Virus after a personal library patch: the original does the same as TI Revive (Reddit report "the next preset is selected back from the ROM/RAM presets, not the personal library ... the original software didn't work this way"). Test (user + Claude): TI Desktop, win10 VM with the original Virus Control 5.1.7 and the Access driver 2.9.66 (drivers only, installed with pnputil from the extracted MSI, no updater), Ableton Live 11, library "OS5 New Features" open in the browser; usbmon capture captures/orig-libstep-1 (180 s, local). Sequence: library patch loaded from the browser -> one single dump to part 1's edit buffer on cable 3 with bytes 10..12 = `20 06 7e` (Flags 0x20, Original Bank/Patch as in the file; E156); then program + + + - on the Virus -> per step the Virus reports `72 00 21 pp` (pp 01, 02, 03, 02) and `74 00 33 3a/39`, and ~0.2 s later the original sends the RAM dump for that slot (bytes 10..12 = `00 01 pp`). The original sends no Multi Part Bank/Patch to the Virus after a library load (no `72 00 20/21` on EP 0x06), so the Virus steps on from the part's previous RAM/ROM slot. User: "It never switched the preset on the library. The virus shows the RAM values". Static (REA/Ghidra, x64 sha256 a622f9a2..): 0x180083ae0 looks up (Multi Part Bank + 1, Patch) via 0x18008daa0 in the numbered device banks only (CVirusBank, RAM/ROM); a non-device patch gets byte 10 = 0x20, a device patch 00/bank/index. TI Revive's load_reported_presets behaves the same; the remaining difference (Flags byte 0x20 for library patches, we send the file's bytes) does not change the +/- behaviour. A web text the user found (2026-10-20, unsourced, reads AI-generated) claims a browser option "Allow hardware control over the librarian patches" that makes PROGRAM/Value step through the library: no such string in Virus Control.dll 5.1.7 x64 (ASCII and UTF-16) nor in the manuals, Addendum and change log of the 5.1.7 installer (pdftotext search); the Addendum only says locked sections stay when browsing "using the hardware or the Virus Control browser" (section locking). Treated as not existing (LOW that it exists elsewhere) | hardware test with the original in the VM, usbmon capture, REA static analysis | captures/orig-libstep-1 (local), native/plugin/source/virusplugin.cpp (load_reported_presets) | HIGH |

| E304 | 2026-10-21 | How the original hands a parameter change to the host without a mouse touch (issue #10: knobs turned on the TI2 are not added in Ableton Live's Configure mode). Static analysis (REA/Ghidra, read-only, sub-agent): the Windows VST3 "Virus TI.vst3" (x64) is a VST2-in-VST3 shim - it loads Common\Virus Control.dll (export VSTPluginMainPocketRocket, 0x180001020) and its audioMaster callback (0x180002850) maps opcode 0 (audioMasterAutomate) to IComponentHandler::performEdit, 43/44 (begin/end edit) to beginEdit/endEdit (handler at wrapper +0x30), 13/42 to restartComponent - no outputParameterChanges. Virus Control.dll is a VST2 AudioEffectX: setParameterAutomated 0x180001690 (setParameter + opcode 0), beginEdit 0x180002eb0 (43), endEdit (44). Class CVSTAutomation (vtable 0x18016f9c0, one slot per automatable parameter, the parameter instance points to its slot at +0x4A0): immediate setters (0x1800666d0, 0x180066630, 0x1800ad240 flag 0) send performEdit only; the deferred setter (0x1800667f0 -> 0x1800ad240 flag 1) queues the slot, and a periodic tick (0x1800ad4a0, GetTickCount, under a critical section) sends beginEdit + performEdit with the current value (one per tick, coalesced) and endEdit once 250 ms passed without a new change. GUI mouse gestures call beginEdit/endEdit directly (0x180027770 / 0x180027800). Patch/bank loads through the CRemoteDevice listener (0x180081980) take a setter that does not reach the automation code (MED: not every call site read). Not determined: which setter a single knob report from the Virus calls (deferred is the plausible one: it exists for changes without a touch, and Live registers the knob) | static analysis of the user's Virus TI.vst3 and Virus Control.dll | - | HIGH (wrapper mapping, AudioEffectX calls, CVSTAutomation tick and 250 ms), LOW-MED (which setter a knob report uses) |
| E305 | 2026-10-21 | macOS: installing a TI Revive package over an installed one of the same version left the plug-in bundles unchanged. MacBook Air M3: the CI package of run 39577249656 (0.8.29, built 2026-10-21 11:34) installed with `sudo installer -pkg` over the 0.8.29 test build of run 39477408077 (installed 2026-10-20): pkgutil receipt 0.8.29, install time 11:48, broker = the package's (sha256 e234fccc..), but VST3 (installed f8c05a04.., package 44e2d989..) and AU (e8a7e696.. vs a7d8a2fe..) still the old ones, file dates 2026-10-20 20:58. Cause: scripts/mac-build-release.sh builds the component plist with `pkgbuild --analyze`, whose default BundleIsVersionChecked = true makes the installer keep an installed bundle whose version is not older (both 0.8.29); only BundleIsRelocatable was switched off. Fix: BundleIsVersionChecked false for every bundle (the package always writes them). Affects test builds with an unchanged version and users who reinstall the same version (e.g. to repair); a newer version replaced the bundles as before (0.8.28 release install matched the package by sha256) | Mac receipt + checksums, package payload unpacked locally Verified 2026-10-21 12:15: the package of CI run 39582553971 (with the fix) installed over the 0.8.29 of run 39577249656 without removing anything replaced VST3 (b3706d1a..) and AU (a3526065..) = the package payload, dates of the new build | scripts/mac-build-release.sh | HIGH |

| E322 | 2026-10-22 | Why the original asks for RAM banks 2-4 twice at start-up (capture orig-startup-lcd-20261022, reply END times; static REA/Ghidra on Virus Control.dll 5.1.7 x64, nothing run). The bank check is a tick state machine: tick 0x18007ec30 -> repository step 0x18008ddd0 (drains the incoming queue, then runs 0x18008e220 when the time stamp CRepository+0x540 is reached) -> bank-info phase: the first bank with 'needs bank info' (CVirusBank+0x129, set for the RAM banks at mount 0x18008ded0) gets 0x18008ea30 = 74 01 7E 07 + 39 bb, next time now + 5 s; asking the same bank again counts as a retry (warning after 3, abort 0x18008e0d0 after 5). The reply entry 0x18008eaf0 accepts any 10/14 reply (267/524 B, byte 7 not 0), forwards it (bank handler 0x18009c150 clears +0x129, hands every program its fingerprint) and sets the time stamp to 0, so the next tick asks at once. One surplus reply therefore makes it ask for the next bank again while that bank's reply (267 B, ~0.5 s on USB) still arrives; the Virus answers both byte-identically and the chain runs to the last bank (each request ~25 ms after a reply's end). The second reply changes nothing (no comparison, same fingerprints); the browser panes are not in the send path. A side effect, not a feature: TI Revive keeps one request per bank (MemorySync::transact, matched by bank, inbox cleared); scripts/startup_diff.py counts the repeats as known. Open: what caused the first surplus 39 02 (22.857 s, no USB input in between; a second delivery of the 14 01 reply suspected, input dispatcher not found) | REA static analysis + usbmon capture | captures/orig-startup-lcd-20261022 (local), native/src/device/memsync.cpp | HIGH (mechanism, timing), LOW (origin of the first surplus request) |
| E323 | 2026-10-22 | How the original follows the DAW sample rate (static REA/Ghidra on the user's Virus Control.dll 5.1.7 x64, tusbcode.bin and the DSP listings in work/firmware, plus the usbmon capture orig-startup-lcd-20261022 with five switches at 52.9/65.3/140.9/180.9/183.0 s; nothing sent). (1) The plug-in decides: VST2 setSampleRate 0x18002fc00 -> device setSampleRate 0x1800a9dc0: the host rate is doubled while below 44000 and halved while above 88000, then >= 46000 gives 48000, else 44100; no rate is refused (88.2/176.4 k -> 44.1, 96/192 k -> 48, 32 k -> 48); the rest is converted by the plug-in's own CSampleRateConverter (0x1800aa1c0, ratios Virus/host and host/Virus in 0x1800a9e90). Applied at the next resume 0x1800a9880 (also at the first start when the rate differs): stop, Sleep(250), IASIO setSampleRate (0x1800fe480; on failure it adopts the driver's rate), createBuffers, converters rebuilt, Sleep(250), start; latency compared and reported (0x18002fc00, MED). (2) Stamps (0x1800a8420) are in Virus frames at the Virus' current rate: the capture's stamped MIDI clock advances 919 frames per tick at 44.1 k and exactly 1000 at 48 k (120 BPM); the fixed constants (lead 1024 frames, reset -100000) are NOT scaled; after every switch the plug-in re-sends the sync ping 0.5-1 s after the stream restart. (3) The driver on the wire: iso stopped (URBs cancelled), SET_INTERFACE alt 0 on interfaces 1/2, ~240 ms, GET_DESCRIPTOR, SET_CONFIGURATION, alt 1/0/1, SET_CUR to EP 0x82, 0x01, 0x82, 0x01 (3 bytes LE), IN iso +5 ms, OUT +70 ms; OUT at 48 frames per packet at once, IN keeps 44.1-size packets ~230 ms; ~0.7 s from stop to 48 k audio; never SET_CUR while streaming; no patches or globals re-sent. (4) USB chip: SET_CUR handler 0x1868 stores a new rate, starts a 250 ms countdown (INTMEM 0x9a, MED) and flags it; main loop 0x2dc sends 4E 73 10 vv to the host CPU (vv 1 for the 48/96 k family, table 0x2f0) and 4E 74 7E 00 (Rise Message 0 = 'Audio clock changed'). (5) DSP: page 0x73 parameter 0x10 handler 0x5205b reprograms the PLL (48 k 0xB40095 at once, 44.1 k 0xB400A2 after 344 ticks), so synthesis and the stamp clock run at the new rate (MED for the reading, HIGH for the code and the 1000-frame ticks). (6) Hypothesis for E175/E241 (LOW): chip routine 0x1b43 applies the global 0x0F 'System Clock/SA Freq' while there is no USB link state, so a Virus with SA Freq = 48 k could switch itself to 48 k after a USB reset (fits E176 2.8 s / E241 3.0 s); to be checked. | E329 | 2026-10-23 | What the original does with notes between an audio restart (rate change) and the new sync marker (static, REA/Ghidra 12.1.4 on the user's Virus Control.dll 5.1.7 x64, sha256 a622f9a2..; for E328). (1) Send path 0x1800a86b0: every MIDI event is sent at once through 0x1800a80f0; a stamp (0xF5 0x53 + 14-bit frame) goes before it only when the timing option flag (object +0xa28, set only by the vtable setter 0x1800aa120, which logs one of two short strings) is on and a device exists - nothing is held or dropped while the timeline is unknown. (2) Stamp 0x1800a8420: stamp = device position (vtable +0x60 of the audio device) - reference (+0xa40) + sample offset x conversion ratio, plus a lead when no marker search runs; the "last stamp" (+0xf74) = -100000 skips the lateness check once. (3) Resume 0x1800a9880 (the restart after a rate change) sets +0xf74 = -100000 but does NOT touch the reference +0xa40. (4) The marker search 0x1800a8900 (input samples beyond +-0x300000) sets the reference +0xa40 = device position + index and ends the search; while it searches, the send path stretches its timeout to 44100 frames on every note-on and forces a sample offset >= 1. Consequence: in the window between the restart and the new marker the original stamps notes against the OLD reference and the restarted device position - its notes there are off as well (how far depends on the driver's position after the restart: not visible statically). TI Revive's two off notes after an in-run switch (E328) are the same kind of transition; holding notes until the new anchor would be our own improvement, not the original's behaviour | REA static analysis | work/rea/virus-control-5.1.7-x64.ghidra12.snapshot (local) | HIGH (send path never holds/drops, reference only set by the marker search, resume leaves it), MED (that the original's notes in the window are audibly off) |

| E331 | 2026-10-23 | Why a Virus can stay at 48 kHz although SET_CUR 44100 is accepted (E175/E241) - static, the USB chip (TUSB3200A 8051, tusbcode.bin) and host CPU bank 0, full instruction dumps from the existing Ghidra projects (read-only; nothing sent). (1) The chip's SET_CUR handler 0x1868 (EP 0x82 or 0x01) compares the requested rate (XDATA 0xfeb1/0xfeb0) with its stored rate INTMEM 0x7e/0x7f; when EQUAL it only acknowledges (bit 0x29) and marks "the PC owns the rate" (bit 0x10): no change flag 0x07, no countdown, nothing to the host CPU (HIGH). GET_CUR (0x0ee8, bmRequestType 0xA2, selector 1 -> 0x1a19) returns a copy of 0x7e/0x7f, the stored value, not a measurement (HIGH). Every chip-side writer of 0x7e/0x7f also sets the change flag (SET_CUR 0x1893, key 0x10 0x0650/0x066b, 0x1b43, link-up 0x03a9, auto-detect 0x022b, init 0x1b82/0x00ee); the main loop 0x02dc then sends 73 10 vv to the host CPU, plus 74 7E 00 ('Audio clock changed') only for a SET_CUR change (bit 0x0c) (HIGH). (2) Divergence path (MED mechanism, LOW that it is the E175 trigger): host CPU FUN_5953 sends its stored global 0x10 (XDATA 0x0410) straight to the DSPs (73 F4 F4 + value 10 40) WITHOUT telling the chip; callers: the reset routine FUN_4ee8 (0x5244) and the deferred job FUN_2bf9 (main loop FUN_1ca8, bit 0x4c set and XDATA 0x4f01 = 0; who sets 0x4c not followed). With 0x0410 = 1 the DSP PLL goes to 48 k while the chip's 0x7e/0x7f still says 44.1 - from then on every SET_CUR 44100 is a no-op and only a power cycle helps: the E175/E241 symptom. (3) Consequence for TI Revive's recovery (ratelever.h, since 0.8.27): SET_CUR 44100 x3 and the global 73 00 10 00 both meet an equal stored value and change nothing; a real switch 44.1 -> 48 -> 44.1 (ratesw.h, on hardware E328) makes the chip announce 73 10 01 / 73 10 00, and chip frames on cable 4 go to the host parser AND the DSPs (FUN_28e7 -> 0x277c / 0x605b, HIGH) - a candidate recovery, untested. (4) 0x1b43 (SA Freq) runs with the USB link UP (byte 0x0c = 0 at link-up 0x039e, 2 at link-down 0x03ca) - corrects E323 (6); it sets 0x7e/0x7f with the change flag, so a later SET_CUR 44100 would work: alone it does not explain E175. Corrections to E257: 0x10b0 is a compare routine against 0x7e/0x7f, not a variable; key 0xFF -> 0x06cd (0 clears bit 0x0e and sets 0x14, else sets 0x0e); unknown keys return. (5) `73 40 10 vv` from the Virus is most likely the DSP echoing FUN_5953's word (part byte 0x40, MED), `73 00 10 vv` the normal parameter path; how 0x3A/0x3B/0x3C route them not found. No message reports a measured rate: the iso IN packet size stays the only real indicator | static analysis (Ghidra headless, read-only) | work/firmware (local): tusbcode.bin, bank00.bin; dumps in the session scratchpad | HIGH (equal SET_CUR ignored, GET_CUR stored, chip writers, cable-4 routing), MED (FUN_5953 divergence, 0x40 echo, 0x1b43 trigger), LOW (that FUN_5953 is what happened in E175/E241) |

| E337 | 2026-10-24 | Crackle in stretches at a large sound-card buffer = the timeline's "jump back to real time" ping-ponging on host jitter (Windows bare metal, Ableton, Creative card at 44.1 kHz / 2048 without ASIO, Virus TI2 Desktop, round-0.8.32 CI test build; follow-up of E187). The plug-in's diag.log 07:44-08:04: Ableton passes 256-frame blocks (`host 44100 Hz block 256 (largest 256)`, as in E187); `drops 0` throughout, process time max 50-260 us and `over block time 0` in real time, `read margin min` never below 0 - so no USB loss, no CPU overrun, no underread. Instead four episodes of about a minute, roughly every 6 minutes (07:44:46-07:45:36, 07:51:31-07:52:21, 07:57:11-07:58:11, 08:03:01-08:04:01): per 10 s 195-587 timeline steps and 2-198 jumps (`anchors`), err -767..852; between them calm (steps 0-3, anchors 0, err -184..256). Two of them with the Virus sounding (raw peaks ~5000-6000); the user confirms the crackle came in stretches. Mechanism (code reading, native/plugin/source/virusplugin.cpp process(), timeline block ~1250-1310): err = (newest ring frame - S) - tl_ is taken from the instantaneous ring position per block; with a marker and abs(err) > 512 the read position jumps by err at once (tl_ += err, the branch made for REAPER's real offsets, E279), else one-frame steps when abs(err) > 256. When the host's block cadence turns uneven, err swings about +-800 frames within a second, so it jumps one way, then back - up to ~20 jumps/s, each skipping or repeating 12-19 ms of audio (a click); the 1-frame steps (up to ~59/s) add small discontinuities. What makes Ableton's cadence uneven every ~6 min is not known (background task, the Creative driver or Ableton). The same day's Windows hil-win.ps1 run (H1, H2-1, H2-2, H3, H6) passed, err within -445..156, no jumps Fix (round-0.8.33, 2026-10-24, native/src/device/timelinefollow.h, used by process()): a jump needs the error beyond 512 on one side for 0.5 s (jump by its mean over that time), or a 2 s mean beyond 512 (an offset hidden in a rough cadence), or abs(err) > 4096 (at once: REAPER processing ahead); one-frame drift steps start when the 2 s mean is beyond 256 and stop at 128. The anchor and the 8192 re-anchor are unchanged. Python model of the same arithmetic (ring in 441-frame chunks, 256-frame blocks; bursts of 8 blocks with +-20 ms jitter and a 40 ms late burst every ~5 s, 60 s): old rule ~3700 jumps and ~3000 steps, new rule 0 jumps and 149-199 steps (8 seeds); a lasting 1000-frame offset: old jumps at once, new one jump after 0.50 s; the same offset in a rough cadence: old ~1930 jumps, new one jump after ~2 s; drift +-200 ppm: no jumps, err -496..485. The model became native/tests/test_timelinefollow.cpp. Linux 2026-10-24 (Virus TI2 Desktop, dev build of round-0.8.33): built (a name clash with the existing follow_ fixed: the member is tl_follow_), 389 unit tests pass; with the old per-block rule put back in the header 3 of the 6 timeline tests fail (rough cadence, jump timing, offset in a rough cadence). | E344 | 2026-10-24 | How the original keeps parts on ANALOG outputs in time with the USB parts: it tells the Virus to delay those parts' audio (static REA/Ghidra 12.1.4 on the user's Virus Control.dll 5.1.7 x64, sha256 a622f9a2.., ~38 calls, background agent; plus wire). (1) The note stamps do NOT depend on a part's output: stamp 0x1800a8420 uses the channel only for per-channel offset bookkeeping (+0xa74/+0xe74); the lead is one global value (+0xa6c, flag +0xa6b; setters 0x1800aa1a0/0x1800aa180, fed from the registry values UseLuxuryLatency / LuxuryLatencyOffset by 0x18006ddf0). (2) 0x180084970 walks the 16 parts: Output Select below 9 (Out1/2/3) = analog; stores per-part analog flags + dirty flags in the device object (+0x1e1119.., +0x1e1129..) and a delay in ms (+0x1e113c; a change marks all parts dirty); 0x18002f9f0 (a vtable method) converts a sample count to ms (samples x 1000 / rate) and calls it; 0x1800a9c20, at the start of every audio block (from process 0x1800aa7c0), sends for each dirty part `F0 00 20 33 01 10 6F pp 7F ms F7` (ms = the delay for analog parts, 0 for USB parts); 0x1800a7770 resets flags and delay at connect. Inference (MEDIUM): the sample count is the plug-in's reported latency (0x180029af0 sets LuxuryLatencyOffset 0xC00 = 3072, copies it to Latency, calls setInitialDelay); the vtable slot of 0x18002f9f0 is not identified. (3) Registry also has AnalogOutputDelay (default 0) with no reader found (maybe unused); 0x18006ed70 sets "Multi Parameters/Output Delay" to 0 for all parts (link to 6F..7F a guess, LOW). Also 0x18002ea50 remaps USB3 parts to USB1 when the third USB output is off. Wire: captures/ref-mastervol-3 (all parts on USB) has the original sending `6F pp 7F 00` 80 times (all 16 parts, value 0, at Play); TI Revive never sends it (captures/trv-analog-1: 0) - so with TI Revive analog parts play undelayed, i.e. early against the USB parts (the user heard analog and USB parts out of sync, 2026-10-24). NON-ZERO on the wire (captures/ref-outdelay-1, 2026-10-24, part 1 already on an analog output, Live 11 in win10ref): the original sent `6F 00 7F 1A` (26 ms) for part 1 and `6F pp 7F 00` for parts 2-16, for all 16 parts at each Play (with 73 00 23 01 + song position) and at Stop (part 1 then 0 - whether because of Stop or an output change is not known); Live's buffer size not noted, so 26 ms is not yet explained by the reported-latency inference. Implemented in TI Revive 2026-10-24 (native/src/device/outputdelay.h: ms = the plug-in's reported latency in ms, clamped to 127; changed parts at once, all 16 at Play/Stop and after a reconnect; 5 tests), first ear test on Linux (REAPER, TI2) FAILED: the plug-in read Output Select by the Snow's name ("pr Output Select Control2/3"); the TI's description names it "Output Select Control2/3" (same wire values 0-8 analog, 9-17 USB), so every part read 0 (analog) and the USB parts were delayed too (diag.log: 16 x 77 ms / 40 ms) - fixed: found through its message (72 pp 29) for any model (test against both descriptions); second ear test (user, REAPER, Linux, TI2, dev build 74c6079d, 2026-10-24 ~17:00): part 1 on Out1 and parts on USB "perfectly in sync" | REA session (not kept in git), captures/ref-mastervol-3, trv-analog-1 (local) | - | HIGH (mechanism, 0-value on the wire), MEDIUM (ms = reported latency), open (non-zero on the wire) |

Seen on the user's Windows PC ("Manuel Schleis Incubation V2" empty), cause found with a Python copy of our logic on the user's files; the same 256 bytes sit in the x86 DLL at init - 0x2a8 (byte compare). How the x86 DLL gets there: "Virus TI Software Suite 5.1.7.00 64-bit.msi" holds both (File keys viruscontrol_dll = x86 in "Program Files", viruscontrol_dll64 = x64; one embedded cabinet #Installer.cab); an ADMINISTRATIVE extract (msiexec /a, what tirevive-access.ps1 and install.sh's Wine fallback do) flattens both trees into one Common and the x86 file wins; msiextract keeps both (x64 next to Virus Control Center.exe, which is PE32+) - checked on Linux 2026-10-24 with msiextract + file(1). Decision (user, 2026-10-24): support only the 64-bit DLL. Change: AccessFiles::open() skips a PE32 Virus Control.dll and names it in the error (Control Center status line, plug-in diag.log); the knob template reader no longer handles 4-byte pointers (E202 path removed); install.sh picks the copy whose DLL is PE32+ and replaces an installed 32-bit one; tirevive-access.ps1 treats a 32-bit DLL as missing and takes viruscontrol_dll64 from the embedded cabinet (msi.dll + expand.exe). Unit tests accessfiles_pe32_refused / _pe32_control_center / _knob_templates_pe32, test_install_linux.py (pe64, access_src). (x64, 14,663,928 bytes); a second run changes nothing (0 s, no temp folders left). A NORMAL Access install (msiexec /i /qn, exit 0) puts the x64 DLL into Program Files\Access Music\Virus TI\Common and the x86 one into Program Files (x86)\...\Common -> full installs were never affected. | E346 | 2026-10-24 | macOS: Virus A/B/C patch libraries load, but with the WRONG default page B. The Mac bundle binary (Virus Control.bundle/Contents/MacOS/Virus Control 5.1.7, universal: x86_64 slice first at 0x1000, i386 at 0x216000) holds the init single at 0x1bcb40 (x86_64); default_page_b() takes init - 0x400 (the Windows x64 layout), which there is 7-bit data that passes our check but differs from the Windows x64 page B in 137 of 256 bytes. The exact 256 bytes of the Windows x64 page B sit in the Mac binary at init + 0x2a0 (x86_64 slice, 0x1bcde0) and again in the i386 slice (0x4448c0). Byte compare on Linux of the user's own files (work/mac payload, msiextract of the 5.1.7 .msi). REA/Ghidra 12.1.4 on the x86_64 slice (thin copy sha256 52bd4a7d.., image base 0, address = slice file offset): the only code reference to 0x1bbde0 (= fat 0x1bcde0) is in FUN_000e5c00, the single dump upgrader; when it grows a 267-byte dump to 524 bytes it copies those 256 bytes into the page B area - the Mac counterpart of the Windows x64 upgrader's page B. FIX 2026-10-24: default_page_b() picks the position by binary format (PE: init - 0x400; thin 64-bit or universal Mach-O: init + 0x2a0); tests accessfiles_bundle_dumps (Mach-O layout with a 7-bit decoy at init - 0x400) and accessfiles_pe_page_b; on the real files (vtimodel pageb) the Mac bundle now gives the same 256 bytes as the Windows x64 DLL.
